<lang>Italian</lang>
<page-type>manuscript-facsimile</page-type>
<meta>This page contains Leonardo's characteristic mirror-writing in Italian, focusing on principles of gravity and a thought experiment involving the weight of a bird in air. There are geometric and mechanical diagrams in the left margin.</meta>

ogni peso ch'è sopeso
liberamēte in esso tira
per la linia del cientro della
sua natura invierso il
cientro del mondo della terra

---

M H
dimanda s'un ucieglo pesi
nell'aria quanto pesi
di sopra in aria sopra alla bilācia
e cosi sotto alla bilancia

---

pesa un ucieglo in una aria
tutta rinchusa in un uaso
e pesa quāto el peso d'esso ucieglo

<margin>
proua della
libra
</margin>

<note>Leonardo investigates the physics of weight and displacement. The top paragraph defines the direction of weight towards the center of the earth. The second and third sections pose a problem about the weight of a bird inside a container or near a balance, a precursor to understanding fluid dynamics and atmospheric pressure.</note>

re che lo dardo linaile
mouendosi e dda ria
mosso effluente dapart-

una parte dello aria

<note>The text appears to discuss the trajectory of a projectile (dardo) in moving air, explaining how the combination of the projectile's motion and the flowing air creates a curved or mixed line of motion. The spelling "linaile" is an old form of "lineale" (linear), and "moffera" is "muoverà" (will move).</note>

<note>Geometric studies concerning the quadrature of the circle and the properties of "lunulae" (lunes), a recurring theme in Leonardo's later mathematical explorations.</note>

v . m . n . o . sono : lune

la &#9723; nela . abula . e . para . &#9789;

<note>The following text block is written to the right of the geometric diagrams.</note>

HO
el b e f n restano dlo
bu di una &#9723; minore e f n
sia lunt una dte para
al resto de la medesma
lunt lunt sono co
dela sofr par al f u
&#9723; e dte abu lunt
ad . . . di

<note>The symbols used by Leonardo represent:
&#9723; = quadro (square)
&#9789; = lunula (lune or crescent-shaped area)
'para' = pari/uguale (equal)
'lunt' = plural of lunula/lune
'sofr' = superficie (surface/area)
</note>

<meta>Manuscript facsimile featuring Leonardo da Vinci's mirror writing in red chalk (sanguine) and a brief note in brown ink. The page contains a technical observation and a corresponding geometric diagram.</meta>

<margin>
In questo libro
</margin>

n . n

quãdo i pūnti m n sō
di pari chore . rispō p mo
to l’un l’altro tãto più
diritto fia n m dū-
que i pūnti n sō f

---

<note>The primary text is written in Leonardo's characteristic right-to-left mirror script using red chalk (sanguine). The transcription above represents the text as read normally from left to right. The text discusses the geometric or mechanical relationship between points 'm' and 'n' and 'chore' (likely a variant of 'corde', meaning ropes or chords).</note>

<note>The diagram, also in red chalk, shows a vertical line with a diagonal arm pivoting from a central point 'm'. The extremities and significant points are labeled with letters (n, m, r, p).</note>

--- Page 17 ---
<lang>it</lang>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<page-num>25</page-num>
<meta>Manuscript facsimile of Leonardo da Vinci's notebook, specifically Codex Forster III, folio 25v. The text is written in red chalk (sanguine) in Leonardo's characteristic mirror script (right-to-left). It describes the anatomical and expressive requirements for depicting a natural-looking cry (pianto) in art.</meta>

### pianto

per fare un pianto
che sia naturale
e non paia riso
bisogna che li
muscoli de la
faccia siano
tutti in moto
e che li ochi
stiano bassi
e le ciglia

<vocab>pianto, naturale, riso, muscoli, faccia, moto, ochi, ciglia</vocab>

<meta>This page from the Codex Forster II features mechanical studies related to bells and striking mechanisms, rendered in red chalk (sanguine) with a small ink annotation.</meta>

<note>The drawings on this folio explore the mechanics of bell-ringing and perhaps the casting or lifting of bells.</note>

campana d'ottone

<note>Leonardo's mirror-script translates to "brass bell."</note>

---

### Description of Drawings

**Top Illustration:**
A detailed study of a bell suspended from a horizontal timber. A complex mechanical linkage is attached to the left side, featuring a series of articulated arms. One arm ends in a hammer or striker that rests against the "waist" or "sound bow" of the bell. This appears to be a design for an automated bell-ringing device or a mechanism to strike the bell without swinging it.

**Middle-Left Diagram:**
A small circular sketch containing a four-armed or cruciform figure. This may represent a schematic view of the bell's interior suspension for the clapper or a plan of the bell's crown.

**Middle-Right Sketch:**
A study of the bell's "crown" or "canons" (the loops at the top of a bell used for hanging it), showing the decorative and functional curves of the bronze cast.

**Bottom Illustration:**
A large red chalk sketch of a vertical screw or spindle mechanism. It consists of a central threaded pole resting on a flat base, with two horizontal arms or levers near the top. This could represent a screw jack for lifting heavy bells or a component of a mold-making apparatus used in the bell foundry.

### Description of Drawings

**Top Drawing:**
A complex mechanical assembly consisting of a horizontal balance beam at the top with weights hanging from both ends. **Bottom Drawing:**
A sketch of a lever or mechanical arm positioned at an angle. To the right, a vertical support structure holds a mechanism that appears to be an escapement or a ratchet-and-pawl system, which interacts with the diagonal arm. A ring weight or handle is attached to the lower end of the arm on the right, while a vertical line (possibly a cord or wire) descends from the upper left end.

--- Page 30 ---
<lang>Italian</lang>
<page-type>diagram</page-type>
<page-num>f. 64r</page-num>
<meta>This page from Leonardo da Vinci's notebooks (likely Codex Forster III) contains mathematical diagrams and notes on the theory of proportions, executed in red chalk (sanguine). The text is in mirror script.</meta>

# COMUNE

---

<note>Diagram demonstrating continuous proportion ($2:4 = 4:8$)</note>

-> 2 <- -> 2 <-
(  ) (  )
-> 2 4 8 <-
-> \_\_\_|\_\_\_ <-
-> | <-

<note>The arcs above the sequence 2, 4, 8 are labeled with the ratio '2'. A vertical line passes through the central term, a common way to visualize the geometric mean in a continuous proportion.</note>

---

<note>Sketch of a circle containing mirror script annotations</note>

-> proportione <-
-> discreta <-

<note>While the top diagram shows a continuous proportion, the label here reads "proportione discreta" (discrete proportion). This likely refers to the second set of numbers below.</note>

---

<note>Diagram of four numbers, likely representing a discrete proportion</note>

(  )       (  )
-> 1 4 10 20 <-

<note>The arcs connect the pairs 1-4 and 10-20. In Leonardo's handwriting of this period, the '4' is often written with a cross-like shape. If intended as a discrete proportion ($a:b = c:d$), one might expect $1:2 = 10:20$, but the character here matches the '4' used in the upper diagram.</note>

<lang>Italian</lang>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<page-num>67</page-num>
<meta>Facsimile of a page from Leonardo da Vinci's notebooks, identified as Codex Forster III, folio 67r. The text is written in red chalk (sanguine) in Leonardo's characteristic mirror script (right-to-left). The content appears to be a list of topics for a planned treatise, covering mechanics (percussion), human motion (leaps), and physiological/philosophical observations on the heart and soul.</meta>

l’obbliquità della p[er]cussio-
ne d’essa p[er]cussione i’ modo
che 'n moto muscholare nō
luogo i o fighu dech-
liberāsi e mediesimamente
commessioni di que' pūti alle
e de modi chome i moto si p[er]de
del moto dell'omo ne' suoi balzi
n'e passaggi de' salti la ragione
del n[ost]ro core E l’anima nō
non basta essa uede se nō
pensa nell’anima di che

<note>The text is written in mirror script. Abbreviations: "p[er]" represents a 'p' with a stroke through the descender; tildes (marked here as macrons or 'nō') indicate missing nasal consonants ('n' or 'm'); "n[ost]ro" is an abbreviated form of 'nostro'. "fighu" is a common Leonardo abbreviation for "figura".</note>

<vocab>obbliquità, percussione, moto muscholare, commessioni, balzi, salti, core, anima</vocab>

<note>This folio contains a collection of disparate red chalk sketches typical of Leonardo's process of thinking on paper. The drawings focus on mechanical components, geometric interlacings, and the dynamics of curvilinear forms.</note>

<note>Upper left and center: Two sketches of mechanical terminals or hooks, possibly related to architectural supports or lifting mechanisms.</note>

<note>Middle left: A vertical columnar study containing a series of repetitive, rounded internal shapes. This may represent a ladder, a sequence of steps, or a mechanical rack component.</note>

<note>Bottom: A series of swirling, curvilinear forms. These sketches resemble Leonardo's numerous studies of natural turbulence, such as water vortices, wind currents, or the organic spiral of hair curls.</note>

<note>The following text is a poetic fragment written in mirror-script (right-to-left) Italian:</note>

> honori de' uirtuosi
> chi ha fede d' uillania
> ma di bugia el fallo ora
> per ragione cosi stanco e
> tu n' adori mio diuino
> S. bastiano

<note>This short verse appears to be a sequence of philosophical or poetic reflections. The final line, "S. bastiano," likely refers to Saint Sebastian, a subject Leonardo sketched and planned to paint on several occasions. The text translates roughly to: "Honors to the virtuous / he who has faith in villainy / but of lie the fault now / by reason so tired and / you adore [it] my divine / S. Bastiano."</note>

vasi di legnio legieri overo di corno in modo
che l'acqua li sostengia offeso dal moto d'essa
chassa mossa per moto di contrappeso della
notte; e 'l m'enpiera della aqua s'intende

<vocab>chassa, calamita, navi, d'ottone, contrappeso</vocab>

# fumo

In p'ma fia . deſonito . el modo . del uedere
ſe . p' mezo . d'eſſo . uedere . ſi conoſcie . p'che
la . luce . ſia . neceſſaria . in tale . effetto . e
maſſimamēte . nell'aria . p' la quale . paſſi

il fumo . anchora . eſſo . fia . neceſſario . ſia
da . uedere . el modo . del darſi . de fumo .
e ſia . ſe p' el ueder ſi conoſcie . chome
li . ſimil fumi . conuerſi . in tenebre . ne la deſtrut
tione . delle . materie . p'cipue . fumo . in fiam
ma . o . fumo . in nebbia . p'che . n'uēgano .
o eſſe . nubi . d'e . fumo . n'uēga . che . conſide
ri . de . fumo . che . ſi lieua . in . aria : conciene . nubi
uēgano . adūque . chōcluxione . uero . ſia . da ſapere
e ueder . chome . tucte . choſe . propoſte . ſia . uarie . uedere
ſieno . conciene . n'uno . parer . uedere . ſia . uero

-> gran parer . uedere . conciene . <-

<note>The term "deſonito" in the first line is likely a variant of "definito" (defined). The word "conciene" appears to be used where "conviene" (is necessary/appropriate) would be expected in modern Italian; Leonardo's 'v' and 'c' can be visually similar in this script.</note>

<lang>Italian</lang>
<page-type>manuscript-facsimile</page-type>
<page-num>f. 72r</page-num>
<meta>This page is from the Codex Forster II. The text is written in Leonardo's characteristic mirror script (right-to-left). The page contains two geometric diagrams and a discussion regarding the definition of straight, curved, and mixed lines in relation to motion between two points.</meta>

chi de' per la riga d'essa linia rittrosa
al dritto d'un dritto ne chostretto d'esservi
che se uno corpo fia mosso infra due termini
breue fia ditta linia infra i ditti termini
la riga d'esta linia se 'l ditta moto fia
fatto infra ditti termini che d'esta riga
e se 'l ditta moto fia fatto per linia torta
chi fia per ditta linia non fia ditta riga
si rimira ancora d'esta riga
d'esta riga che per linia fia ditta
e se 'l ditta moto fia fatto per linia
mista e fia mista la ditta riga
esta riga fia ditta riga in ogni ditta
a ditta linia

---

The text discusses the mechanics of cords (ropes) wound around these cylinders, specifically investigating how cord thickness and the number of windings affect speed or tension. d'una doppia . chorda . chome qui . vedi apresso .

del moto circulare o uer angolare
il peso che discende per l'aria
tira tanto più tardo quãto è
di più aria occupatone . perche
laria lo rispignie

se . d . s . f . fusse d'oro . d'arzento . di ferro

# Transcription

<note>This page consists entirely of diagrams and sketches related to the physics of arches and the distribution of weight. There is no sustained prose, only numerical annotations and symbols.</note>

<margin>
15
</margin>

<note>The diagrams represent various configurations of curved beams (arches, bow-like structures, or quadrants) with weights, represented by squares, suspended from them. Leonardo uses these to study tension and equilibrium.</note>

---

*   **Top Right Diagrams:** Two sketches of curved horizontal members with weights suspended from their centers. The weights are marked with the numeral **8**.
*   **Center and Marginal Diagrams:** Various arches of different curvatures and orientations (concave up, concave down, or vertical segments). Many are marked with a small cross symbol (**+**) at the vertex of the curve or at points of attachment.
*   **Bottom Left and Right Diagrams:** More complex arrangements involving pulleys and intersecting curves to redirect the force of the suspended weights.

<vocab>arco, peso, tensione, contrappeso, equilibrio</vocab>

<note>Study of a "perpetual motion" wheel. Leonardo analyzes the movement of weights on articulated arms, ultimately concluding that such a device cannot provide continuous motion because the center of gravity will always settle at the lowest point (equilibrium).</note>

Qualunche peso fia applicato al rote
il quale peso sia causa del moto dessa
rota sanza dubbio il cientro di tal
peso si fermera sotto il cientro del
polo dessa rota e nessu moto fia
che fia possibile dasmouere desto
sito segli non saggiugne desso peso

Ancora sauessi uno peso il quale
ogni punto uscissi della sua rote
e tornassi entrasse in essa rote quello
si fermera ancora infra il polo e lo
rizzonte di sotto e non si partira
mai per nessu moto di tal rote se
nuovo peso non saggiugne

il moto che fia partorito dalla forza la quale
sia equale al peso fia tanto piu veloce
chel peso fia minore . l'aria fia quella
che tiene el peso . ancora fia quella che
lo sostiene con le sue fatiche . el peso
che fia piu grosso sarà mantenuto dalla
sua aria per più spatio . e farà essa grosseza
chel moto fia tardo rispetto alla proportione
del peso medesimo se fussi di minore area .

è la sua potentia . la quale fia in el
diritto della percussione aria contro aria
e tanta aria fia spinta in drieto quanta è
quella che per uantaggio d'aria fia
spinta da n a m .

--- Page 198 ---
<lang>Italian</lang>
<page-type>text</page-type>
<page-num>15</page-num>
<meta>This manuscript page consists of Leonardo da Vinci's mirror-script text, specifically a repetitive permutational study of mechanical and structural principles. The text explores relationships between "ponte" (bridge), "legnami" (timbers), "forza" (force), and "vituperio" (failure/structural injury), using abbreviated symbols for weight (p), length (l), force (f), and distance (d). The handwriting is extremely regular, arranged in rhythmic, almost tabular paragraphs.</meta>
<warning>A large brown stain obscures a significant portion of the left half of the page. Some text is faded or blurred within this area, but the repetitive nature of the lines allows for a consistent transcription.</warning>

+ pōte · lēgnami · dēlla · forza · dēlla · vītuperio · vētē ·
lēgnami · vērā · mō · dēlla · vērā · d · p · l · d · f · l · p ·
mō · dēlla · f · d · p · mō · dēlla · f · d · vērā · pōte ·
pōte · dēlla · lēgnami · dēlla · f · d · p · mō · dēlla · f · d ·

<note>This text is a moral reflection on how individuals with less intelligence or "wit" (ingiegnio) tend to be more presumptuous and critical of others' work. Leonardo uses mirror writing throughout. The abbreviations with tildes indicate missing nasal consonants (e.g., "māchamēto" for "manchamento", "tātū" for "tanto"). The abbreviation "p ." is commonly used by Leonardo for "per".</note>

<note>This page appears to record experimental observations or instructions for cooling liquids in vessels (vasi) using wet cloths (panni). Leonardo repeats the word 'rinfrescha' (cools) throughout, testing different arrangements of materials. The terms 'verno' (winter) and 'gielo' (ice/frost) suggest he is comparing these methods to natural cold. The word 'grigna' might refer to a specific location or perhaps the Grigna mountain, or it could be a technical term.</note>

# 55

<note>The following text is written in Leonardo's characteristic mirror script (right-to-left) and is transcribed here in normal left-to-right reading order, preserving original spelling and abbreviations.</note>

Tāto è più la gravità de' pesi e' quali
si movā per linia obliqua che quello
che si move per linia perpendiculare

quāti i pesi n'è più lūgho el bracço
della bilancia che lo sostiene tāto è più
la gravità o vogliā dire el suo peso
perpendiculare che lo sostiene dālla parte

cō questi dvo' pesi o māchādo l'ū o l'altro
vogliā vedere quale sia la potētia de' decti
pesi

<note>This page from Leonardo da Vinci's Manuscript A (folio 5v) contains philosophical and technical reflections on the necessity of conciseness in scientific and artistic treatises. Leonardo argues against overly long volumes that clutter the mind, advocating instead for clear, necessary demonstrations, specifically mentioning the importance of perspective in painting. The mirror writing has been transcribed as normal left-to-right Italian text, preserving Leonardo's original abbreviations (such as tildes for nasal consonants) and archaic spelling (e.g., 'āchora' for 'ancora', 'dēti' for 'denti').</note>

# de gravità

<note>Leonardo’s mechanical studies on the law of the lever, illustrating the principle of inverse proportionality between weights and their distances from the fulcrum. In these diagrams, Leonardo explores how different weights produce equivalent moments (torques) when positioned at specific distances.</note>

<note>Diagram 1: A horizontal lever with a central fulcrum 'a'. Three weights are illustrated at different positions: 2 units at distance 6, 4 units at distance 3, and 6 units at distance 2. Each combination results in a balanced state (2 × 6 = 12, 4 × 3 = 12, 6 × 2 = 12).</note>

<note>Diagram 2: A similar study using weights of 1, 4, and 8 units. Weight 1 is at distance 8, weight 4 at distance 2, and weight 8 at distance 1 ($1 \times 8 = 8, 4 \times 2 = 8, 8 \times 1 = 8$).</note>

<note>Diagram 3: A compound lever system where one bar supports another, demonstrating the distribution of weights (1, 2, 4, and 8) through a multi-stage mechanism.</note>

### Drawing Description
The diagram at the top of the page illustrates a mechanical problem involving a horizontal lever or balance beam. 

- **The Beam and Weights:** A horizontal beam is depicted with five weights suspended from it. The weights are represented as squares and labeled with the numbers **4**, **1**, **5**, **3**, and a final symbol resembling a cross (**+**).
- **Proportional Markers:** Above the beam are several vertical lines of varying heights, marked with numbers and letters (including **5**, **5**, **m**, **e**). These likely represent the distances from a fulcrum or reference points for calculating moments.
- **Mathematical Annotations:** Smaller numbers (**1**, **1**, **m**, **1**, **2**) are placed along the vertical axes. Thin curved lines beneath the beam indicate the potential arc of movement or deflection.
- **Calculations:** In the right margin, Leonardo has written a sequence of numbers (**1-1-4-11**) and a fractional calculation (**21/5**) that relates to the sum of weights or distances shown in the diagram.

<note>This page contains Leonardo's diagrams and notes on the physics of motion, gravity, and the mechanics of weights on levers. The diagrams represent early studies into the law of squares for accelerated motion.</note>

<note>The first diagram at the top depicts a horizontal bar with units marked '1'. Curved lines descend from these markers to a vertical axis on the right, which is annotated with the sequence of square numbers: 1, 4, 9, 16, 25.</note>

<note>The second diagram shows a lever-like horizontal beam with weights hanging in boxes. The boxes are labeled '9' and '36', corresponding to the square of their respective distances (3 and 6 units) from a central point.</note>

<note>The third diagram illustrates two concentric arcs, possibly comparing different curvilinear paths. The larger arc is labeled 'o', 'd', 'o' and the smaller 'm', 'n', 'm'.</note>

il moto fatto per righa retta è di natura
differente da quello che si ffa per linea
curua circulare

---

ogni peso che si muoue per l'aria si
parte in . 4 . parti chonsiderate da
tute le potenze che lo muouo cioè
lo sforzo e la forza della motora e
il peso della chosa mossa

<vocab>moto, righa retta, natura, linea curua circulare, peso, aria, potenze, sforzo, forza, motora, chosa mossa</vocab>

### [Proportional Diagrams]

che s’andera corta fia el lūgo p la meza
e corta fia quella p lo dretto fā mouere

<note>Geometric diagram showing a horizontal beam from which four vertical cords (labeled f, e, d, a) are suspended. These cords support two overlapping catenary curves. Numerical labels, appearing as fractions (1/2), are written alongside the vertical cords.</note>

<margin>
se l'peso è
1 e fia .4.
corde ogni
corda ne
fia 1/4

se l'peso è
2 fia ogni
corda 1/2

e s'el è 1/2
fia 1/8

e s'el è 1/4
fia 1/16
</margin>

Tanto è la uirtù della corda .f. e . quanto
è quella che la sostiene . se .f. è .1/2. e .e. è
con .f. essa fia .1. però fia .1/2. alla .1/2.
e medesimamente fia .1/4. alla .1/4.
perchè la uirtù della corda è diuisibile
per le sue proportionali grauezze . d .
però se .f. e . a . son .1/2. e . e . d . son .1/2.
fia il peso .1. posato sopra .4. uolte .1/4. e .4.
uolte .1/4. è .1. e se il peso fusse .2. allora
fia .1/2. per ogni .4. e se il peso fia .3. allora
fia .3/4. per ogni .4. e se il peso fusse .1/2.
allora fia .1/8. per ogni .4. e se fusse .1/4.
allora fia .1/16. per ogni .4. e se fusse .1/8.
allora fia .1/32. per ogni .4. e se fusse .1/16.
allora fia .1/64. per ogni .4. e così procedi
all'infinito . sempre fia il peso diuise
per .4. e della uirtù d'una delle corde fia
manifestamente el tutto il peso <unclear>diuiso</unclear>

dice la uoce si sparge per l'aria · in ogni parte · circularm̅te

<note>Leonardo's notes on acoustics. He describes sound ("la voce") as spreading through the air in concentric circles ("circularmente"). He notes that as the air's motion moves further from the source ("origine"), it eventually ceases. The strike-throughs and subsequent revision show Leonardo refining his thought on the movement of sound waves relative to their source.</note>

<vocab>uoce, aria, circularmente, moto, origine, muoua</vocab>

# meccanica

<note>Diagram of a lever or balance beam. A horizontal beam is suspended at a fulcrum 'c'. To the left at point 'n' hangs a weight labeled '5'. To the right are points 'd', 'a', and 'f'. 'a' is the midpoint between 'd' and 'f'. Points 'd' and 'f' have small marks under them, representing weights. The diagram illustrates a study on the center of gravity and lever equilibrium.</note>

questi pesi che sono in d e f s'unischino
quelli in d e f fano .5. e sono nel
mezo di d f . el peso .5. che è in
n è di tal quantità come il peso
che è in d f . e per questo il peso
unito fia d'opposta parità al peso
n e 'l centro di tal peso fia in a e
a esso a el peso fia pari al peso n
perche el braccio n c è pari al braccio c a

---

<margin>
<note>Stylized capital letter 'P' or a diagrammatic symbol marking a new proposition.</note>
</margin>

la metà del braccio di n fia
come d f raddoppiati in a e fia
la metà del braccio di n raddoppiato d e a
li n i due d'essi pesi fia pari al peso
in n e se raddoppiato fia d'opposta parità
esso braccio fia pari al peso n
ed è n nel suo braccio d'opposta
parità s'egli fia pari a d c . ma se
el peso n fia raddoppiato fia in
perpetuo d'i tal peso raddoppiato
el peso fia in a e fia pari
al peso raddoppiato in n
perché lo raddoppiato d c è
pari a n c

# meccanicha

<note>Diagram of a lever balance with a central fulcrum and a weight suspended from the left arm.</note>

li pesi ch'anno i rami delle bilance
sono in contraproportione a essi
rami dal polo de la bilancia chome
al peso del quale esso polo n'è mezo
infra il polo e 'l peso e 'l polo s'allontana
dal peso el quale peso è gravezza el
polo fia di lungi quanto fia el rami
delle bilance infra el polo e 'l peso
ma l'oncia pesa el polo
